You are reviewing one ticket in this repository (La Hoja Boricua, a vanilla JS PWA in site/ on Cloudflare Pages, D1 behind Pages Functions). Review only; do not edit files. Do not load or run any skills. The worktree is C:/LHB/lhb-105-wt (branch lhb-105-switch, cut from main bfdfeae, which carries LHB-80, LHB-98 and LHB-104). TIER 2, round 4.

Ticket LHB-105: a pass started in another tab during an account switch survives the copy change. Found by you on LHB-80 round 6 (evidence/lhb-80-review-r6.txt, finding 1): isolate() writes the copy's new epoch, then parks and clears the copy and sets the new owner, all outside the sync lock. A pass that starts in another tab after the epoch write and before the clear captures the new epoch, stays alive() across the clear, reads the clear as deletions and uploads empty collections.
Done-when: no pass that starts during a switch writes the copy or uploads, in a two-engine test, and the switch still completes. Proof: that test seen red before the fix; the full node suite with 0 fail.

Reproduced on main, before the fix (evidence/lhb-105-red-main.txt): two engines share one storage, as two tabs. The other tab (Y) starts isolating to B and stops halfway: in the test the clear throws, after everything isolate writes before the clear. The shared account returns to A and this tab's pass starts; while its GET is held, the other tab clears the copy and stops before setOwner. Measured on main: the pass returns ok with pushed true at rev 2, and the server copy of A is empty (humidor []), which is your measured wipe.

The fix (site/sync.js only):
- isolate() writes a switch mark before it touches the epoch: one key per tab, lhb_mihoja_v1_cambio.<tab>, value {v:1,t:<nowMs>}, where <tab> is 10 random characters fixed per engine. The body that was isolate() is now iso1(), unchanged. After iso1 returns (true or false), isolate removes its own key. If the mark cannot be written, isolate returns false and nothing else is touched. If iso1 throws, the key stays and lapses after SWITCH_TTL (30 s), which models a tab that closed mid-switch.
- switching() scans the keys with that prefix and is true while any well-formed mark is within SWITCH_TTL of now (either direction). A read that throws propagates, and once() turns it into fail 'storage', as the other storage reads there do.
- once0() checks switching() right after it reads ep0, and returns fail 'switching' (retried by run()'s backoff) while a mark is fresh.
- alive() is unchanged. Reasoning: the mark is written before the new epoch, and the pass checks the mark after it reads ep0. So a pass either read the new epoch (the mark was already there, so it does not start, unless the switch already ended, when the copy and owner are consistent) or read the old one (the new epoch kills it at its next alive()). A pass alive before the switch began is already stopped by the epoch, as on main.
- Contract impact: a new localStorage key family, lhb_mihoja_v1_cambio.<tab>, with a version field v:1. Nothing else reads keys under that prefix (hoja.js reads only lhb_mihoja_v1_de_*, the owner and the exit fence).

Tests (three added, named LHB-105):
1. Your interleaving as above. With the fix: the pass does not succeed, and neither do its retries while the other tab is halfway. No PUT, the copy is not written by this tab (setDocs unchanged), and A's account stays at rev 1, whole. Then the other tab completes its switch to B: the owner is B, no switch mark is left, B syncs ok, and nothing of A reaches B's account.
2. A switch whose mark cannot be written (storage refuses the prefix): isolate returns false, and the owner, copy and epoch are unchanged, with nothing parked.
3. A tab that closed mid-switch: past SWITCH_TTL, the next pass runs and A's account stays whole.
Evidence: evidence/lhb-105-mutations.out, evidence/lhb-105-tests.txt (tallies in the commit message; your sandbox's LHB-44, LHB-45 and LHB-73 failures are Python and temporary-directory denials).

Round 1 (evidence/lhb-105-review-r1.txt) blocked on three; all fixed in round 2 (site/sync.js and two lines of site/hoja.js):
- R1-1 (P1): a tab asleep mid-switch past the 30 s lapse could resume and clear under an epoch a new pass had captured. A mark now counts while it is recent OR while its tab is alive: every engine takes a Web Lock named lhb_mihoja_v1_pestana.<tab> for its life (hoja.js: D.tabLock requests it with a promise that never settles; D.tabsHeld returns the names navigator.locks.query() reports held, or is null without Web Locks). switching() is now async: with tabsHeld, a mark whose tab lock is held counts at any age; a mark that is neither recent nor held is a closed tab's, and it is removed. Without Web Locks, only recent marks count (the round 1 behaviour). And a switch that wakes after another tab switched does not go on: iso1() checks right before the clear, and again before setOwner, that the epoch is still the one it wrote; if not, it returns false and leaves the copy alone.
- R1-2 (P2): once0() now removes its own leftover mark, then awaits switching() BEFORE it calls isolate(), so a pass never switches the copy while another tab is mid-switch; it checks again after reading ep0, as before. isolate() itself (which hoja.js calls before reading or writing Mi Hoja) refuses while another tab has a recent mark, so opening Mi Hoja does not switch the copy either.
- R1-3 (P3): a mark that could not be removed is kept as unmark and removed before this tab's next switch or next pass. A tab ignores its own mark (marks() skips its own key), so its own passes are not held back; other tabs wait until it is gone.
Six tests added (named LHB-105 (Codex r1)); on the round 1 code (evidence/lhb-105-r1-red-before.txt) 9 tests, 4 pass, 5 fail, the five new behaviours below; the sixth (a switch that begins exactly when the pass reads its epoch, injected at that read) guards the check after the epoch read, which round 1 already had: your R1-1 sequence with a live tab past the lapse (no PUT, no copy write, A whole; then the tab closes, the mark is removed and the pass runs); your R1-2 sequence (the pass and a direct isolate() both leave the copy and owner as they were; the other tab then completes and the pass runs); your R1-3 removal failure (the other tab waits; the switching tab's next pass removes its mark; the other tab then runs); a switch asleep after parking while, past the lapse, a third tab switches to C and C writes, after which the first switch wakes and returns false with C's copy intact; a pass of another account while a live tab sleeps mid-switch past the lapse (the copy and owner stay); and the epoch-read injection. Six mutants added for these (evidence/lhb-105-mutations.py, 11 in all).

Round 2 (evidence/lhb-105-review-r2.txt) blocked on three; all fixed in round 3 (site/sync.js only):
- R2-1 (P1): without Web Locks, an expired mark was dropped while its tab might still be alive, and nothing stopped that tab from resuming its clear. Now the pass that removes a mark whose liveness is unknown (no tabsHeld) first writes a new epoch (once per pass, newEpoch()); if that write fails, the mark counts as live and the pass waits. A tab that wakes after that point finds a different epoch at still() and does not clear. This is the sequence you measured (pause after parking, 31 s, pass, resume during GET): the test acts out what is left of the sleeping switch (its still() check and its clear) at the GET, because one thread cannot resume a call that threw; it expects that check to see another epoch, no PUT, and A whole.
- R2-2 (P1): isolate() now refuses while any mark is live by the same rule the pass uses, not only a recent one (liveNow): recent, or, with Web Locks, a tab whose lock the last lock query reported held, or ANY mark while no query has answered yet (isolate is synchronous and cannot ask; every engine starts a query at load, and every pass that finds a mark refreshes it). So with locks, Z cannot switch to C while Y sleeps mid-switch past the lapse, so Y cannot later clear C's copy; after Y's tab closes and a pass has queried the locks, Z switches. Tested: Z refuses with Y alive; a fresh Z2 whose lock query never answers refuses; after the lock is released and a pass runs, Z3 succeeds.
- R2-3 (P2): the mark is removed in a finally block, so a switch that throws removes it too (or sets unmark if removal fails, as before). Tested with an exception at the clear: no mark is left and the other tab syncs.
Residual, stated rather than fixed: without Web Locks, a tab whose switch passed still() and then stopped for more than 30 s before its setDoc({}) (the next statement) could clear a copy another tab switched in meanwhile. iso1() is synchronous, and browsers freeze or discard tabs between tasks, not inside one, so this needs the OS to stop the renderer mid-task for 30 s. LHB-98's D.lock is also a no-op without Web Locks, so the same browsers already have weaker sync exclusion. With Web Locks the case is closed by R2-2.
Test model note: in these tests a throw stands for a tab asleep mid-switch. Since the mark is now removed in finally, the helper captures the mark inside the throwing hook and puts it back after the throw, as it was when the tab stopped.
Three tests added (named LHB-105 (Codex r2)); on the round 2 code (evidence/lhb-105-r2-red-before.txt) 12 tests, 9 pass, 3 fail: exactly the three new ones. Four mutants added (evidence/lhb-105-mutations.py, 15 in all, 15 of 15 red in evidence/lhb-105-mutations.out): the epoch takeover removed; isolate by recent marks only; an unanswered lock query read as no live tab; removal outside finally. Full suite (evidence/lhb-105-tests.txt): tests 468, pass 467, fail 0, skipped 1.

Round 3 (evidence/lhb-105-review-r3.txt) blocked on two; both fixed in round 4 (site/sync.js only). (A first round 3 run died after 15 s with no verdict; it is kept as evidence/lhb-105-review-r3-killed.txt.)
- R3-1 (P1): cleanup of an expired mark could remove a mark the same tab wrote for a new switch under the same key. Now every switch marks a new key, lhb_mihoja_v1_cambio.<tab>.<n>, with n counting that tab's switches; a key is written once and only removed after, so its value never changes: whoever saw an old mark can only remove that same old mark. marks() reads the tab from the key (up to the dot) and skips this tab's own keys; unmark now holds the key that could not be removed. Your sequence is a test: Y switches to B and cannot remove its mark; past the lapse, this tab's pass (no Web Locks) writes the takeover epoch and, right before removing the old mark, Y starts a switch to C and stops before the clear; the pass removes only the old key, sees Y's new mark at its second check and does not start (no PUT, no copy write), and Y's new mark is still there.
- R3-2 (P1): a lock query from before a tab opened could call that tab closed. Now a lock query only decides that a non-recent mark's tab is closed if the query started after the mark stopped being recent (heldAt >= mark.t + SWITCH_TTL); otherwise the mark counts as live. A tab takes its lock when its engine is created, before any switch, so a query that starts 30 s after the mark was written sees that lock if the tab lives. askHeld records the time it started and keeps the newest answer. Your sequence is a test: Z opens and queries before Y exists; Y switches to B, and right before Y's clear the lapse passes and Z opens Mi Hoja in C: Z returns false and Y completes.
Test model note: with one key per switch, the helper's finish() stands for the sleeping call that wakes and completes: it finishes the switch and removes the mark it started with (its finally). Two earlier tests use it instead of a second isolate() call.
Two tests added (named LHB-105 (Codex r3)); on the round 3 code (evidence/lhb-105-r3-red-before.txt) 14 tests, 12 pass, 2 fail: exactly the two new ones. Two mutants added (17 in all, 17 of 17 red in evidence/lhb-105-mutations.out): every switch of a tab marks the same key; a lock query from before the mark went stale still decides. Full suite (evidence/lhb-105-tests.txt): tests 470, pass 469, fail 0, skipped 1.

What to look for: an interleaving in which a pass that starts during a switch still writes the copy or uploads; a way the mark blocks sync longer than a switch takes (other than a tab that dies mid-switch, which lapses at 30 s); a failure path of isolate that leaves the mark or loses the LHB-80 guarantees (the epoch put back only while it is still the one written there, the parked copy, the owner); the per-tab key colliding or being removed by another tab; anything in the tests that could pass while the rule is broken. Web Locks across tabs are an option the ticket leaves open, not a requirement. Block only on a defect this branch introduces, or on one within LHB-105's scope that should be fixed before release.

Report each finding with file, symbol, a concrete failing scenario, and whether it blocks. End with exactly one line: VERDICT: PASS or VERDICT: BLOCK.

+function guagua(cls=''){
+ // v8: la guagua es la ilustración B que escogió Sergio (turquesa y crema, sin logo), recortada en WebP con transparencia.

+function rs(R,k=.5){let p='';for(let i=0;i<5;i++){const a=-1.5708+i*1.2566,v=(b,r)=>(r*Math.cos(b)).toFixed(1)+' '+(r*Math.sin(b)).toFixed(1);p+=(i?'':'M'+v(a-.6283,R*k))+'Q'+v(a,R*1.25)+' '+v(a+.6283,R*k)}return p+'Z'}
+function flor(cls=''){const k='f'+(++n),u=(id,x,y,a=0,s=1,m)=>`<use href="#${k}${id}" transform="translate(${x} ${y})${m?' scale(-1 1)':''} rotate(${a}) scale(${s})"/>`;

+function flamboyan(cls=''){const k='b'+(++n),q=i=>{const x=Math.sin(i*91.7)*4e4;return x-Math.floor(x)},B=[[16,52,11],[30,44,14],[48,37,16],[70,34,17],[92,37,16],[110,44,14],[124,52,11],[40,54,11],[62,55,12],[84,55,12],[104,54,11]];let t='';

 <h1>¿Qué artesanos de cigarros hay en cada pueblo de Puerto Rico?</h1>
 <p class="answer">La Hoja Boricua tiene 32 artesanos de cigarros. 26 tienen pueblo o zona en la guía, en 19 pueblos y zonas: Adjuntas (1), Aguas Buenas (2), Área metro (1), Caguas (4), Cidra (2), Coamo (1), Humacao (1), Isabela (1), Juana Díaz (1), Lares (1), Mayagüez (1), Ponce (1), Río Grande (2), San Juan (1), Santa Isabel (2), Toa Alta (1), Toa Baja (1), Trujillo Alto (1) y Villalba (1). De 6 artesanos la guía no indica el pueblo.</p>

 <h1>¿Qué es un cigarro hecho en Puerto Rico?</h1>
 <p class="answer">En La Hoja Boricua, un cigarro hecho en Puerto Rico es uno torcido en la isla. 2 de los 32 artesanos de la guía tienen una fuente que lo confirma: <a href="../artesanos/don-chamorro-ponce.html">Don Chamorro</a> y <a href="../artesanos/kikes-cigars-villalba.html">Don Kike Cigars (Kike&#x27;s Cigars)</a>. Para los demás, dónde se tuercen sus cigarros todavía no está verificado.</p>
